Published ranges are stated in cases for commercial screening. For trays and crates, the rate, the loaded weight and the platform are validated per application (product, end-of-arm tool, pallet pattern, reach and process).
When two platforms could both work,
TRF
recommends the one that matches your throughput, production intensity and growth plans, not simply the lowest initial investment. When packing, conveying and palletizing are all in scope,
end-of-line integration
delivers them as one project with one partner. Compare every model on the
robotic palletizing page
.
Questions
Frequently asked questions
Can a Tray Palletizer Work in a Freezer or a Chilled Room?
Yes. For palletizing areas below 32°F,
TRF
Subzero
is the
TRF
palletizer built for frozen food, ice cream and other below-freezing areas. For a chilled room above freezing, send
TRF
the room temperature so the application can be evaluated. In every case, rate, payload and tooling are validated per application.
Can One Tray Palletizer Handle Several Tray Formats?
Yes, within the validated application. Each format and pallet pattern is stored as a recipe, so the operator selects the pattern at changeover. The practical limits are the tooling's ability to hold every format securely, the robot's reach at the tallest stack and the number of pallet positions the layout allows, all confirmed during project engineering.
What Is the Difference Between a Tray Palletizer and a Tray Packer?
A
tray packer
loads packaged trays into shipping cases; a tray palletizer stacks trays, or the finished cases, onto pallets. They sit in different parts of the line and often work together: a case packer packs the trays, a
case palletizer
builds the pallet of cases, and conveying connects them.
What Payload Does a Palletizing Robot Need for Loaded Trays?
The robot must carry the loaded tray plus the tooling, and for multi-tray picks, the combined weight of the trays. Published case weights are up to about 22 lb for
TRF
Cobot
, 44 lb for
TRF
Compact
and 66 lb for
TRF
Speed
.
TRF
validates the payload with your heaviest loaded tray and evaluates a custom palletizer above that range.
Can a Tray Palletizer Be Used in a Protein Plant?
Yes, for packaged trays and crates. Packaged trays move between machines on
TRF
Intraflow
, and palletizing below 32°F points to
TRF
Subzero
. Sanitary conveying for exposed product upstream is evaluated per application with
TRF
Cleanflow
. Each application is validated with the plant's tray formats, cleaning routines and room conditions.
How Much Does a Tray Palletizer Cost?
Cost depends on the platform, rate, loaded tray weight, tooling, pallet positions, pallet handling and the conveying around the cell. As a reference, list prices are US$ 120,000 for the
TRF
Cobot
Dual, US$ 180,000 for the
TRF
Compact
Dual and US$ 350,000 for the
TRF
Speed
100, EX Works Lenexa, Kansas, installation not included. The
robotic palletizer cost page
explains each factor.
What Information Should I Prepare to Size a Tray Palletizer?
Bring your tray and crate formats with dimensions, the loaded weight of the heaviest format, trays per minute at peak, shifts per day, the room conditions (chilled, frozen or washdown), stacking rules and a drawing of the floor space. A
TRF
Sales Engineer uses this data to screen
TRF
Cobot
,
TRF
Compact
,
TRF
Speed
or a custom palletizer, and the
payback calculator
gives a first estimate.

your tray and crate formats with rates and stacking rules, and a Sales Engineer will validate the tooling, the patterns and the reach with your trays.

Plant management and engineering
How does the palletizing ROI calculator estimate payback?
The
palletizing ROI calculator
divides the system cost, including 20 years of maintenance, by the annual labor cost of manual palletizing minus the annual electricity of the robot. The labor cost comes from the people who palletize on each shift, hours per shift, shifts per day, days per week and an hourly labor rate. The rate starts from a reference production wage for your state, based on public U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics data, and you can edit it.
Electricity uses public U.S. Energy Information Administration state rates with a 20 kW reference draw, and maintenance is 5% of the investment per year. The result is an initial planning estimate, not a quote, and a
TRF
Sales Engineer completes it with the costs of your project. The same calculator appears on the
TRF
Cobot
,
TRF
Compact
and
TRF
Speed
pages, with that platform already selected.
The robotic palletizer cost guide
The
robotic palletizer cost guide
is the written companion to the calculator. It explains what moves the investment, from platform tier and payload to end-of-arm tooling, pallet patterns, conveyors, pallet handling, controls, integration, installation and training, and it lists the reference prices of the three standard platforms. Use the guide for the narrative of your capital request and the calculator for the numbers inside it.
What data do you need before estimating palletizer payback?
Most of the inputs already exist in your production and payroll systems.
Data group
What to collect
Why it matters
Production
Shifts per day, hours per shift, days per week, average and peak cases per minute
Sets annual operating hours and the platform range
Labor
People dedicated to palletizing per shift and your loaded cost per hour
Drives the labor cost the palletizer replaces
Product
Unit type, dimensions, weight and number of SKUs
Decides tooling and platform fit
Pallet
Pallet type and size, patterns, load height and slip sheet use
Affects cycle time and tooling
Plant
Floor space, layout drawing, ceiling height, forklift flow and operating temperature
Determines footprint and any special construction
Financial
Payback hurdle, budget range and approval calendar
Frames how the request is presented
If part of the list is missing, start anyway. Unit weight, rate per line, shifts and a photo of the available area are enough for a first screening conversation.
How do you build a capital request for palletizing automation?
A capital request is stronger when it presents the problem, the options, the full installed cost and the payback method. Cover these six things:
The problem in operational terms:
operators per shift, repetitive lifting, rate limits and the effect on shipping.
The options considered:
keeping the process manual, a collaborative cell, a compact industrial cell, a high-performance platform or a
custom palletizer
.
The full installed cost:
system, tooling, conveyors, pallet handling, controls, integration, installation and training, not equipment price alone.
The payback method, not just the result:
the formula, the labor source and the inputs you used.
The benefits outside the payback:
ergonomics, capacity, consistency between shifts and SKU flexibility, labeled as benefits that were not priced.
The implementation and support plan:
project stages, the installation window and support after startup.
Questions
Frequently asked questions
Is the Palletizing ROI Calculator Free to Use?
Yes. The calculator is free, and the preview appears as you type. A work email unlocks the full breakdown, and
TRF
uses your email and inputs to follow up on your project.
How Accurate Is a Payback Estimate Made Before a Quote?
It is an initial planning figure. It becomes sharper with your own inputs, above all your loaded labor rate, the number of people who really leave the task and an investment that includes installation and integration. Costs such as other utilities, financing, taxes and layout changes are not in the estimate, so a
TRF
Sales Engineer completes it.
Can I Estimate Payback for Case Packing or Conveyor Projects?
The calculator is built for palletizing, and the same labor logic applies to case packing. For a
case packing
or
conveyor systems
project, talk with a
TRF
Sales Engineer about your products, rates and layout, and the engineer will build the estimate with you.
What Is the Next Step After I Run the Numbers?
Contact a
TRF
Sales Engineer with your product weight, rate per line, shifts and a sketch of the available area. The engineer confirms the platform, defines the scope and prepares a quote with the full installed cost for your line, replacing the reference values in the estimate with validated ones.

This calculator compares your current manual labor costs against the total cost of owning and operating a robotic system over its 20-year lifespan. The analysis includes the system purchase price, maintenance (5% annually), electricity costs, and the scale of automation needed based on your workforce size, following the method of the A3 (Association for Advancing Automation) Robot System Value Calculator. Labor data from the U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics; electric data from the U.S. Energy Information Administration (2024 to 2025). Figures are undiscounted and exclude inflation, financing and taxes.

How does the calculator estimate payback?
The calculator follows the method of the A3 (Association for Advancing Automation) Robot System Value Calculator and compares the labor cost of manual palletizing with the cost of owning and operating a robotic system over a 20-year period, the period used by the A3 method:
Manual labor per year
= people per shift × hours per shift × number of shifts × days per week × 52 weeks × hourly labor rate.
Adjusted system price
= reference price × the scale of automation for your workforce (1 to 2 workers per shift: 1x; 3 to 4: 1.5x; 5 to 6: 1.95x; 7 to 9: 2.5x; 10 or more: 3.3x).
Electricity per year
= annual operating hours × your location's electricity rate × a 20 kW reference draw × an electricity multiplier of 1x (1 to 2 workers), 2x (3 to 4), 3x (5 to 6), 4x (7 to 9) or 5x (10 or more).
Total system cost
= adjusted system price + maintenance at 5% of that price per year for 20 years.
Break-even, in years
= total system cost ÷ (manual labor per year minus electricity per year).
20-year labor savings
= manual labor over 20 years minus the robotic system over 20 years (total system cost plus 20 years of electricity).
The method is simple and undiscounted, so anyone can rebuild it in a spreadsheet and carry the annual figures into a net present value model.
Which inputs move robotic palletizer payback the most?
Test these four inputs one at a time:
Shifts.
The investment is paid once while the labor savings repeat on every shift, so payback falls as shifts increase.
People per shift.
Count only the people who would leave the task. A realistic figure protects the credibility of your request.
Labor rate.
The location reference is an average production wage. Replace it with your actual rate when you know it.
System cost.
A reference list price without installation and integration makes payback look shorter than it will be; use Custom to test your quote.
Questions
Frequently asked questions
What Is the Typical Payback Period for a Robotic Palletizer?
There is no single typical figure. Payback depends on the labor hours the palletizer replaces, shifts per day, local labor cost and the cost of the system you buy. The calculator applies the method on this page to your inputs, and a
TRF
Sales Engineer completes the estimate with the installed scope of your project.
Where Does the Reference Labor Rate Come From?
The reference hourly rate for each state is a production wage from public U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics data, a base wage without payroll costs. It fills in when you select your state, and you can edit it at any time. Use your own loaded rate, including benefits, when you present the estimate to finance.
Is the Calculator Result a Quote?
No. The result is an initial planning estimate built from your inputs and reference data. Your final price and payback come from a quote, which a
TRF
Sales Engineer prepares after validating the platform, tooling, pallet patterns, conveyors, installation and integration for your line.
Can I Use This Calculator for a Case Packer?
The calculator is built for palletizing, and its platform list covers

TRF

. The labor logic is similar for case packing: the manual labor removed, compared with the cost of the system. For
